Group 1 (Market)

The Stoxx Europe 600 closed at 664.1 on Friday 13-Nov, its highest close of the last 12 months: up 2.4% in the week, 2.7% over the fortnight, 3.7% over the month, 1.9% over three months and 14.4% over twelve months. The bases: the week is the 5 sessions from the 6-Nov close, the fortnight 10 sessions, the month 21 sessions, as on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard; industry figures are price moves of each industry's index over the same sessions (the Market chart, 3 and 12 months). Since the 30-Oct month-end the index is up 2.7%; since the previous note's readings (10-Nov) it is up 0.9%, most of it on Wednesday 11-Nov (+0.7%), with Thursday (+0.1%) and Friday (0.0%) nearly flat.

  • The three sessions since 10-Nov: Transportation (+2.7%), Professional, business and consumer services (+1.7%), Infrastructure (+1.2%) and Consumer staples (+1.1%) led; Defence fell (4.5%), and Real assets (0.4%) and Consumer discretionary (0.3%) slipped. Industrials and capital goods rose 0.1%, 0.8 points behind the index.
  • The week: Financials +2.6%, Technology +2.6%, Professional, business and consumer services +2.5% and Transportation +2.4% led a broad rise; Real assets (+0.2%), Media (+0.3%), Consumer discretionary (+0.5%), Healthcare (+0.6%) and Defence (+0.6%) lagged.
  • The month (21 sessions): the leaders are Healthcare +7.4%, Consumer staples +5.8%, Infrastructure +5.8%, Professional, business and consumer services +5.1% and Real assets +5.1%. The laggards: Energy, commodities and metals mining (1.9%), Technology (1.4%), Defence (0.5%), Transportation (0.4%) and Industrials and capital goods (+0.6%, 3.1 points behind the index).
  • Three months: Consumer staples +9.2% and Healthcare +9.0% lead, then Infrastructure +7.2% and Professional, business and consumer services +5.9%; Defence (12.3%), Real assets (3.6%) and Media (2.8%) lag. Twelve months: Transportation +43.6% and Energy, commodities and metals mining +35.6% lead; Media (10.1%), Real assets (4.3%) and Defence +4.6% lag.

The pattern for the book: a defensive rotation under a rising index, with Industrials and capital goods left behind. Over the month the defensive industries (Healthcare, Consumer staples, Infrastructure) gained 5.8% to 7.4% while Industrials and capital goods gained 0.6%. Four of the book's holdings sit in Industrials and capital goods (Volex, Arteche, Sdiptech and IMCD; Alfen too until its sale on 11-Nov), and all four carry its Industry RS of 26, below the 30 line, at Friday's reading. That is why one Sell Criterion, Industry RS below 30, gives a sell signal on Volex, Arteche and Sdiptech at once (and would on IMCD as a Core Investment): it is one industry reading, not four stock readings. What would refute it: Industrials and capital goods outperforming the index over the next two Fridays, which would lift Industry RS back over 30 for Sdiptech at the 20-Nov reading.

  • Defence was the one heavy faller of the three sessions since 10-Nov: (4.5%), though +0.6% over the five-session week and (12.3%) over three months. Theon (Defence) fell with it, which is part of why its 200-day line exit came on 11-Nov; the cause of the industry's fall is not in the sources read this week.
  • The consumer and staples holdings rode the rotation: Carlsberg's Sector RS (87) and Industry RS (79) reached 13-month highs, yet Carlsberg itself lagged AB InBev, Heineken and Diageo, which closed at 52W highs; Hilton Food lagged Consumer staples over the month (+3.3% against +5.8%); Fevertree made a 52W closing high on light volume.
  • Telecoms (+1.3% week, +4.5% month) turned up after months of underperformance, as your 13-Nov note observed ("Most of the Telcos are rebounding"); Helios Towers, which the data file places in Infrastructure (+5.8% over the month), ran far ahead of both with ten up closes in a row (+17.1%).

The Portfolio Fitness for Fighting assessment of the ideal portfolio (the target table of 13-Nov-2026, set by the Assistant Portfolio Manager, for your decision): the regime is read as risk-off beneath an index at its 52W high, weakening at the margin for the first time. Breadth broadened for a third week (63% of stocks above their 200-day averages), cyclicals led defensives over one week while defensives still lead over four weeks to six months. The tilts: Consumer staples Overweight; Consumer discretionary and Real assets Double Underweight; Telecoms, Defence and Media Underweight; the rest Neutral, Industrials and capital goods included; momentum Underweight and fragility Double Underweight. Two rows changed letter this week: Energy, commodities and metals mining from Overweight to Neutral, and the Electrical equipment, grid and automation sector from Neutral to Underweight. The logic in one line: own quality defensives with low fragility, avoid crowded momentum, and wait for the rotation to confirm before adding cyclical risk. The book today is long Industrials and capital goods (Sdiptech, IMCD, and Volex and Arteche until their 17-Nov sales), which the table holds at Neutral, and long Defence through Theon until its 17-Nov sale, which the table holds at Underweight at industry level (Theon's sector, Defence - Non-primes, at Neutral); its largest holding, Helios Towers (7.92%), sits in Infrastructure, held at Neutral, but in the Infrastructure - telecom towers sector, held at Underweight; and Arteche sits in Electrical equipment, grid and automation, the sector moved to Underweight this week.

Your market notes. On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Index moving higher on narrow breadth" and "Defensives trade". The data since then partly confirm it: the index rose a further 0.9% to a 12-month closing high, and defensives still lead over the month; but breadth has broadened for a third week (63% of stocks above their 200-day averages), so "narrow" is less true than it was on 10-Nov. Your "AI + Industrials trade" has faded: Industrials and capital goods is up 0.6% over the month, and your 13-Nov note calls Nexans, NKT and Prysmian "a fairly fragile topping Stage 3 pattern". Your "Energy + oil trade" has also faded: Energy, commodities and metals mining is down 1.9% over the month, and the target table moved it to Neutral this week.

4. Consensus by named FY

Volex's consensus rose about 1% on the results and the SP fell 7.1%: estimates and SP have moved opposite ways for five weeks. FactSet, US dollars, seven analysts; the year ends in March.

  • FY2027 (year to Mar-2027) EPS: 52.68 cents, +4.1% in one month, +16.3% in three, +35.4% in twelve.
  • FY2028 EPS: 58.34 cents, +3.4% in one month, +15.3% in three. FY2029 EPS: 64.42 cents, +3.1% and +14.1%.
  • Revenue (FY2027 $1,420m) about +2.3% in one month and +7.4% in three; EBITDA (FY2027 $195.2m) about +3.1% and +11.5%, for all three years.

The revisions came in two steps, both on Volex's own news, the second less than half the size of the first. From 15 to 19-Oct (the trading update) FY2027 EPS rose 2.9%; from 11 to 13-Nov (the results) 1.2%, with revenue +0.6% and EBITDA +1.4%; every series was flat in between. The earnings review (12-Nov): most brokers had "already upgraded in October".

The later years rise slightly more slowly than FY2027: analysts are lifting the near year, not the trajectory. Implied EPS growth is 10.7% from FY2027 to FY2028 and 10.4% to FY2029; the implied EBITDA margin (EBITDA over revenue) is steady at 13.7%, 13.9% and 14.0%.

Consensus sits below the company's second-half guide. FY2027 revenue of $1,420m less the reported first half of $723.6m implies about $696m for the second half, 3.8% below the first, against a guide of "broadly flat sequentially" once the roughly $10m pulled forward is offset by a full half of Kepler SignalTek (earnings review). Hypothesis: the market is pricing a data-centre plateau into FY2028 that the estimates do not show. Test: the earnings review's triggers, a second-half revenue more than $15m below the first half's run rate, or a second-half underlying operating margin below 10.5%. Your Stage 3 checklist fits: "Earnings momentum + earnings beats are a red herring; it normally looks good in early Stage 3".

  • The 200-day line rule: the 200-day MA of the share price and its stop-loss (the line less 6%), the whole holding sold under the 10-day stop-loss rule {MA}
    • Where it stands: Sell signal: exit confirmed Wed 11-Nov on a €27.36 close at or below that day's stop-loss (about €29.55); not yet sold.

Hypothesis 1 (better supported): a thin stock with an overhang of shares bought at €32 or more (about 3.03m from the June placement at €33.00 and 1.05m traded from 17-Sep to 21-Oct, Deep Dive Memo) meets sellers on any rise, and its holders sold into the group's bounce. Hypothesis 2: a company-specific negative not yet public; the Deep Dive Memo finds none in the files, and investor relations and the brokers know of none (management's word). Test: a day on which Arteche rises on 1.5x its 50D average while Legrand and Siemens Energy fall would be the first sign of decoupling upwards; Arteche falling on Pfisterer's 18-Nov orders while Pfisterer rises would add to hypothesis 2.

Who is cutting, and their stated logic (earnings review of 22-Oct; Placera, 19-Oct):

  • Nordea cut its PT to SEK 435 from 465 and DNB Carnegie to SEK 425 from 430 on 19-Oct, both keeping Buy. 4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts are raising Sdiptech's FY2027 and FY2028 and cutting its FY2026 EPS: the trajectory beyond this year is rising.

  • FY2026 (year to Dec-26): EPS (8.0%) since 30-Sep to SEK 13.16, in two steps, (5.4%) on 30-Oct and (1.1%) on 5-Nov, while FY2026 revenue rose 2.9% and EBITDA 3.1%.
  • FY2027: EPS +2.1% since 30-Sep and +7.9% over 3 months, to SEK 17.79; revenue +5.2% and +6.8%, to SEK 5,647m; EBITDA +2.8% and +4.0%, to SEK 1,416m.
  • FY2028: EPS +3.7% and +9.2%, to SEK 19.97; revenue +5.9% and +7.5%, to SEK 6,047m; EBITDA +3.4% and +4.6%, to SEK 1,523m.
  • The trajectory: implied growth from FY2027 to FY2028 rose from 10.6% (30-Sep) to 12.3% for EPS, 6.4% to 7.1% for revenue and 7.0% to 7.6% for EBITDA: analysts are raising the trajectory, not pulling earnings forward.
  • The margin: the implied FY2027 EBITDA margin is 25.1%, from 25.7% at 30-Sep; revenue is still raised faster than EBITDA, as in the 28-Oct note.

Hypotheses for the FY2026 EPS cut while FY2026 EBITDA rose, ranked: (1) a basis effect: the A&J memo (31-Oct) notes Sdiptech shows three EPS figures and FactSet four bases, and the 30-Oct step came the day a sixth analyst entered the EPS count, which went back to five on 5-Nov; (2) items below EBITDA, such as the SEK 13m early bond redemption charge in the third quarter (earnings review). Test: the fourth-quarter report on 10-Feb-2027.

The links. Sdiptech's SP led the estimates up into 14-Oct and has lagged them since: FY2027 revenue consensus is up 4.0% since 13-Oct while the SP fell 3.6% from its high. The A&J memo's (31-Oct) plausible downside is FY2027 EPS of SEK 17.1, 3.9% below consensus; its tests are the next two quarters' organic EBITA growth and the Supply Chain & Transportation margin back above 20% by the second quarter of 2027. The 5-Nov step fits the Sellside Analysts' Memo's (3-Nov) view that estimates rise "in steps as acquisitions arrive".

Sdiptech's first non-Buy rating in 24 months came on 30-Oct with a seventh analyst, who appears to sit well below the average PT. Every covering analyst rated Sdiptech a Buy at every month-end from October 2024 to September 2026, through its fall and rise: the ratings carry no signal. The average PT fell from 312.7 (six analysts, 26 to 28-Oct) to 308.1 (seven, 29-Oct); seven times 308.1 less six times 312.7 implies a new PT of about SEK 281, 8% above the close (a hypothesis: it assumes the other six were unchanged). The analyst's name and logic are not in the sources read.

Sdiptech's average PT has followed its SP for 24 months, and the gap has widened as the SP stalled. The average PT fell 35% from 398.8 (August 2024) to 258.4 (January 2026) and has risen 19% since, to 308.1: 18.1% above the SP, from 12.6% at the 14-Oct high. 3. RS

Xvivo's Stock RS has turned up from the bottom of its range, from 8 on 4-Nov to 15 at the 13-Nov Friday reading: a bounce inside a strong sector, not yet a turn.

  • Stock RS: 15, from 9 at 6-Nov, 53 on 16-Oct and 92 on 14-Aug (down 38 points over 1 month and 77 over 3; up 13 over 12 months, from 2 on 14-Nov-2025); 55 points below the line of 70 and 35 below the line of 50.
  • Sector RS (medtech consumables): 83, from 82 at 6-Nov, 67 at the 30-Oct month-end and 23 on 9-Oct; 33 points above the line of 50.
  • Industry RS (Healthcare): 92, unchanged from 6-Nov, from 26 on 7-Aug; 62 points above the line of 30.

The order of the turn is stock-specific selling inside a sector rotation, the opposite of a rotation carrying the stock. Xvivo's Stock RS fell first, from 89 at 28-Aug to 37 at the 30-Sep month-end, while Sector RS sat between 21 and 41. Sector and Industry RS then rose from 16-Oct while Xvivo's Stock RS fell from 53 to 8, through the 22 to 27-Oct estimate cuts; all four cohort peers' Stock RS rose over the month (Vitrolife from 22 to 76, Ambu from 39 to 79).

This week the link to the sector loosened in Xvivo's favour: Healthcare rose 0.6% and the Stoxx Europe 600 2.4%, Xvivo 4.3%; Sector RS gained one point and Stock RS six. Hypothesis: October's stock-specific sellers are exhausted (item 2 (Volume)), so Xvivo's Stock RS can rise as the healthcare bid cools. Against: six points from 9 is noise at the bottom of the range.

Precedent: the last time Xvivo's Stock RS was this low (February to mid-March 2026), Xvivo led its sector out, from 12 on 13-Mar to 56 at the 31-Mar month-end and 90 on 17-Apr, with Sector RS between 12 and 30. Today the sector lift came first and has not reached Xvivo. Confirms a turn: Stock RS above 50 at a Friday reading, which in March took under three weeks.

4. Consensus by named FY

Xvivo's third-quarter report moved profit out of FY2027 and FY2028 and into FY2026: FY2027 EPS is down 12.1% in a month and FY2028 EPS down 7.6%, while FY2026 EPS is up 5.9%. Financial years to December, in SEK.

  • FY2026: EPS SEK 3.701, up 5.9% over 1 month (from SEK 3.496 at 30-Sep) and 5.3% over 3 months; revenue SEK 988.6m, (0.5%) over 1 month; EBITDA SEK 202.1m, (2.1%) over 1 month and (10.3%) over 6.
  • FY2027: EPS SEK 5.308, (12.1%) over 1 month, (13.1%) over 3 and (19.7%) over 6; revenue SEK 1,201m, (4.3%) over 1 month; EBITDA SEK 301.3m, (11.7%) over 1 month.
  • FY2028: EPS SEK 8.75, (7.6%) over 1 month and (12.6%) over 6; revenue SEK 1,595m, (0.9%); EBITDA SEK 489.9m, (4.1%).

The cuts are to margin, not revenue, and they push the earnings path out a year. FY2027's implied EBITDA margin (EBITDA divided by revenue) fell from 27.2% at 30-Sep to 25.1%, against the company's 2027 target of an EBITDA margin above 30%; FY2028's fell less, from 31.7% to 30.7%. Implied EPS growth from FY2026 to FY2027 fell from 72.8% to 43.4%, while growth from FY2027 to FY2028 widened from 56.9% to 64.8%. Analysts are pulling earnings forward into the year already almost reported and pushing the recovery onto FY2028 and the United States heart launch, the pattern the 28-Oct note recorded from May to August.

The FY2026 rise is below the EBITDA line and of low quality. The one estimate change since the previous run, on 12-Nov (daily series; the key-days table dates it 13-Nov), raised EPS for all three years (FY2026 +1.6%, FY2027 +1.2%, FY2028 +0.7%) while cutting FY2027 EBITDA 0.8% and leaving revenue unchanged. The earnings review of 22-Oct explains the gap: third-quarter EPS of SEK 1.42 included about SEK 7.1m of exchange-rate gains, and DNB Carnegie's 2026 rise was on financial income and tax (items 5 and 6 (Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth)). The Sellside Analysts' Memo of 3-Nov forecast exactly this: "a mechanical lift to financial year 2026 earnings that flatters momentum, then cuts to financial year 2027 at each of the next four reports, a cumulative 25 to 35%".

Xvivo's SP led its estimates down. On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Price fell 20%+ over the prior 2M. Ergo, earnings just confirmed fundamentally what people suspected or were whispering about". The data agree: the SP fell 30.1% from 14-Aug to 7-Oct, before the October cuts; over 3 months FY2027 EPS fell 13.1% and the SP 26.8%, twice as far.

The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's test is already passed on the downside. The memo of 26-Oct expected FY2027 consensus to settle near SEK 5.5 once Pareto and Stifel update, then fall toward SEK 4.0 to 4.5 by the third-quarter 2027 report, against its own SEK 3.82; consensus is SEK 5.308. Xvivo's cohort peers moved the other way: over 3 months one-year-forward EPS rose 11% at Surgical Science and 7% at Bonesupport and Vitrolife, and fell 2% at Ambu, against Xvivo's (13%).

Test: FY2027 EPS at the 30-Nov month-end; a further fall toward SEK 5.0 would confirm the memo's path, while a flat reading would be the first pause in the cutting since June. The next real test is the fourth-quarter report on 28-Jan-2027 (confirmed).

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Xvivo's ratings carry no information about this move: 6 Buys of 6 at every month-end from October 2025 to 13-Nov-2026. Buys rose from 4 of 5 at the September 2025 month-end to 6 of 6 at October 2025, after a 51.7% fall; the count of analysts has not changed since. The ratings follow valuation, not momentum: they rose after the 2025 fall and have not moved through this 33.3% fall.

Net Upgrade Breadth is at or near its worst on every measure but EPS.

  • Revenue: (86%) at 13-Nov, 6 of 7 cutting and none raising, as at the 31-Oct month-end; from +83% at the May month-end and 0% at 30-Sep.
  • EBITDA: (100%), all 7 cutting, unchanged since 31-Oct; from +83% at the May month-end.
  • EPS: (67%) at 13-Nov, 1 raising and 5 cutting, from (100%) at 31-Oct: the first raise since September, from the 12-Nov update (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • PT: (57%), 4 of 7 cutting and none raising, unchanged since 31-Oct; from +71% at the April and May month-ends.
  • The divergence: EPS breadth improved while EBITDA breadth stayed at (100%), the below-the-line lift of item 4 (Consensus by named FY), not an operating upgrade.

Where analysts are cutting, and their stated logic. Two of the seven are named in the sources read; the others' reasons are not in the sources read.

  • DNB Carnegie (Buy, PT SEK 330, held since July 2026) raised 2026 adjusted EPS 10% to SEK 3.9 on financial income and tax, and cut 2027 4% to SEK 5.7 and 2028 3% to SEK 8.6 "for lower projected abdominal sales", not for the largest customer, and argues "the LungBio loss is overdone, offset by the OPO hub expansion and price initiatives" (OPO: organ procurement organisation) (Investment Analyst's earnings summary, 30-Oct).
  • Redeye cut its base-case fair value from SEK 385 to SEK 360 and moved to 90% discounted cash flow and 10% peer multiples "to reflect LungBio uncertainty", sizing the largest customer's in-house move at 5% to 6% of 2027 group sales (same source); on 23-Oct it blamed worries that Lung Bioengineering's own system "could create a revenue headwind in 2027" (Finwire via Börsvärlden, in the 28-Oct note).
  • Redeye expects margin pressure in the second half of 2027 from United States launch spending, and management (Rosenblad) warned on the 22-Oct earnings call of "a slight margin dip in late 2027" (earnings review, 22-Oct); this fits FY2027's implied EBITDA margin falling to 25.1% (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)). DNB Carnegie's view on 2027 margins is not in the sources read.

Xvivo's average PT (SEK 324.9 since 28-Oct) is 57.7% above the SP, narrower than the 64.6% of 26-Oct because the SP has risen and the PT has held.

  • The average PT fell 2.9% on 6-Oct (to SEK 336.1) and 2.3% on 28-Oct (to SEK 324.9): (3%) over 1 month, (6%) over 3, (7%) over 6, from SEK 360.0 at the May month-end.
  • At the 13-Aug high the gap was about 12%; at the February 2026 month-end it was 72.2%, with PTs falling, and Xvivo's low came two sessions later; at the March 2025 month-end it was 69.2%, also with PTs falling, and the SP fell a further 42.9%.
  • The gap alone has not marked Xvivo's lows. What matters is whether PTs stop falling: no average-PT change for 12 sessions since 28-Oct.

Test: revenue and PT Net Upgrade Breadth at the 30-Nov month-end; any raise in revenue estimates would be the first since the May month-end.

9. Peers

Xvivo is still its cohort's laggard, but this week it stopped falling on its peers' bad days. Over 1 month Xvivo is (9.2%) against +4.5% to +25.4% for its four cohort peers (the peer table), but the week to 13-Nov reads differently:

  • 10-Nov: Xvivo +1.6% while Bonesupport fell 3.0% and Vitrolife 1.2% (chart files).
  • 12-Nov: Surgical Science fell 10.8% and Demant 8.3% on heavy volume (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)); the cohort averaged (3.1%) and Xvivo (1.6%). The causes of those falls are not in the sources read.
  • The week: Xvivo +4.3%, behind only Ambu (+4.6%); Bonesupport +0.2%, Vitrolife (0.7%), Surgical Science (8.4%). Hypothesis: the October pattern, Xvivo falling on its cohort's up days (22, 23 and 26-Oct), has ended, and the stock now trades on its own supply, which has dried up. Against: one week, on light volume. Test: whether Xvivo holds SEK 193 if the cohort falls again.

Your four peer checks.

  • Sell-side estimates: over 3 months one-year-forward EPS rose 11% at Surgical Science and 7% at Bonesupport and Vitrolife; revenue Net Upgrade Breadth is +40% to +100% across the four peers against Xvivo's (86%). The Sector Digest's hypothesis of 28-Oct, that in this cohort the shares follow the direction of estimates more than the reported quarter, still holds.
  • SP technicals: every cohort peer is above its 50D and absolute 200D MAs, where Xvivo is 7.8% and 15.2% below them; Surgical Science's 8.4% weekly fall on heavy volume is the first crack in a peer's trend.
  • Newsflow: your weekly AlphaSense report was not read, so peers' news since 6-Nov is not in the sources read; the last dated development is Bonesupport's 22-Oct beat on United States sales (Redeye, Finwire via Börsvärlden), when it rose 24.3%.
  • Company guidance and communications: nothing new since the 22-Oct reports is in the sources read; Xvivo kept its 2027 targets (EBITDA margin above 30%) in July and October 2026, moving the framing to "2027 as a springboard to 2028" (Geography Digest, 4-Nov).

Xvivo trades with no group. Its highest correlations outside the cohort are weak (Demant and Vimian 0.26, Verisure 0.24), and the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo of 26-Oct finds 91% of its variance its own. The wider sector is strong (Demant Stock RS 97, Sonova 92, Getinge 90), which holds medtech consumables' Sector RS at 83; the Sector RS test (information only for a Speculative Bet) needs a 34-point fall by 30-Nov.

What the peers imply. The memo puts a sector recovery's worth to Xvivo at only 5% to 8%, and the peers' rise has passed Xvivo by for a month. The more useful signal is 12-Nov: if Surgical Science's fall starts a de-rating of Nordic small medtech, the stock that has already de-rated may hold best, and Xvivo's light-volume 1.6% fall that day is the first evidence for it.

What the rules say for Xvivo now, and what decides the next step

The findings that matter most

  • The sale risk is the 10-day stop-loss rule on the 20-day line, and Monday 16-Nov's close weighs more than usual. Both counted closes (12 and 13-Nov) sat in the bottom half of the day's range (0.12 and 0.44), so a third close at or below the line in the bottom half of its range confirms the exit.
  • The falling 20-day line works for the holding, but not yet on Monday. As the late-October closes of SEK 223 to SEK 232 drop out, a close above about SEK 206.1 on Monday, SEK 204.8 on Tuesday or SEK 203.5 on Wednesday clears the line and ends the test (my calculation from the chart file). An unchanged SP of SEK 206.0 on Monday 16-Nov falls just short of the line (SEK 206.1) and would count as day 3; if that close sat in the bottom half of the day's range it would confirm the exit, sold at the Tuesday 17-Nov session. An unchanged SP clears the line from Tuesday 17-Nov.
  • The selling has dried up, but no buyer has shown (item 2 (Volume, and relative price against its 52W high)); FY2027 EPS is still being cut (12.1% in a month).

What the rules say for each tranche today: hold. Tranches 1 and 2 (Restarting Reinforcing / S3 Speculative Bet, 0.42% and 0.58% of capital) are testing, day 2 of 10 (first close at or below the line on 12-Nov): the 20-day line SEK 207.41, the stop-loss SEK 194.96, the close SEK 206.0, 0.7% below the line and 5.7% above the stop-loss. No sale is due at the Tuesday 17-Nov session on the 13-Nov readings; the loss at the stop-loss is about 0.05% of capital. Your 11-Nov ruling, "Xvivo = Set to S3 Speculative Bets in the PMS", is applied to both tranches.

The nearest sell tests, and how they interact.

  • The exit is confirmed on the first of: a close at or below the stop-loss (about SEK 193.7 on Monday at a flat line, a 6.0% fall); a third bottom-half close at or below the line (Monday at the earliest); three lower closes in a row at or below the line (Monday to Wednesday at the earliest); the 10th close at or below the line (Wednesday 25-Nov). A confirmation on Monday 16-Nov sells both tranches at the Tuesday 17-Nov session; one from Tuesday 17-Nov to Monday 23-Nov sells at Tuesday 24-Nov.
  • How fast: the line has applied only since 12-Nov, so there is no 1, 4 or 13-week history on it; Xvivo has moved 1.7% a day on average over ten sessions, against 0.7% to the line and 6.0% to the stop-loss.
  • One fall, several signals: a close below SEK 193 would confirm the exit, break the 3 to 6-Nov shelf and take relative price to a new low; the Sell Criteria (information only) would deepen, not change.

The record. Twice in this decline Xvivo regained its 20D MA and lost it again: on 26-Aug (lost two sessions later) and 15-Oct (lost six later, on 23-Oct, on 2.18 times average volume). The 14-Jul tranche exit came in a pullback that recovered to SEK 309.0; the 14 and 21-Sep exits came in the decline, and Xvivo is 18.4% below its 14-Sep close.

For your ruling

  • Your 13-Nov intention against the rules' verdict: on 13-Nov you wrote: "As a test of alignment, I think we need to sell Sell Alfen, Volex, Xvivo, Arteche, Theon. I want to rebuy Arteche, Volex, Theon and Xvivo if/when Speculative Bet criteria allows." The rules give no sale at Tuesday 17-Nov unless Monday's close confirms the exit. Your 12-Nov "EXIT THE POSITION as a FAILED Broadening / Early S2 Probing Bet?" matches the rules' count, narrowly (the August high was 4.96% above the May high, so that base never completed). A re-entry as a Restarting Reinforcing / S3 Speculative Bet has its early indicator (5D and 10D MAs flattening) and late indicator (5D MA turned up since 10-Nov), and needs a trigger day. Your 10-Nov "re-attack if wished as S1 Probing Bet (not valid ATM IMHO)" still reads not valid: the daily classification for new capital is Restarting Reinforcing / S3, not Bottoming / S1.
  • The line was set while the SP was below it: the 20-day line took effect on 11-Nov, when Xvivo closed at SEK 206.0, 1.9% below its 20D MA (SEK 209.91); the rules let a line be chosen "only when the price is above that moving average on the day of investment". This was a relabel, not an investment. Counted from Xvivo's first close below the line (23-Oct), the exit would have been confirmed that same day: the 23-Oct close of SEK 206.0 was below the stop-loss of SEK 207.0 (the 20D MA of SEK 220.20 less 6%).

The next step. Every close reads the 10-day stop-loss rule; Friday 20-Nov reads Industry RS and the relative 200D MA (information only). The rules block an add: 2 tranches are held, the most a Speculative Bet holds. Where Xvivo stands for an add: none of the three stages (not pulling back or basing; not Retesting a rising MA or forming a volatility contraction pattern; not healthily Retesting or breaking out). The Master Dashboard reads pulling back 1 of 4, basing 2 of 4, Healthy Retest 0 of 4, volatility contraction pattern 0 of 4; the nearest is basing. No add trigger is present. Dates: the regulator's next letter on the United States heart application, due early January 2027 (the memo); the fourth-quarter report, 28-Jan-2027. What would change this reading: a trigger day through SEK 210, or a close below SEK 193. Richard decides.

D'Ieteren

Restarting Reinforcing / S3 Speculative Bet · 2.49% of capital in 1 tranche (tranche 1, 2.49%, bought 12-Aug-2026) · readings as at Fri 13-Nov-2026

1. SP and MAs

D'Ieteren fell 1.6% in the week to 13-Nov, to €175.7, while the Stoxx Europe 600 rose 2.4% and its industry, Consumer discretionary, 0.5%: after beta (1.18) its own part was (4.5%), 1.2 times its usual weekly move of 3.7%. On 13-Nov it closed below the 20D MA (€175.97) for the first time since 8-Oct, which starts day 1 of 10 of the 10-day stop-loss rule on tranche 1. Over 3 months D'Ieteren is up 1.9% and over 12 months up 21.6%; it is 10.1% below its 10-Feb closing high (€195.44).

Developments: your weekly AlphaSense report could not be read this run, so the Developments come from the earnings review, the research filed and the data file; none of them dates company news between 4 and 13-Nov.

D'Ieteren's last two legs: an industry-carried rally to a lower high, then its own fall against a rising market.

  • 24-Sep to 4-Nov, +14.3% to €182.8 (29 sessions): the first part, to 8-Oct, was D'Ieteren's own: it rose 2.2% on 2.7x on 8-Oct against an industry down 0.8%, the day Bloomberg News reported that Belron had picked four banks for a listing that could come next year (Bloomberg Línea, 8-Oct, in the 28-Oct note). The last part, 28-Oct to 4-Nov (+7.3%), was the industry's: Consumer discretionary rose 2.1% on 2-Nov and 2.5% on 4-Nov as its Industry RS went from 26 to 53, with unchanged estimates (item 4 (Consensus by named FY)).
  • 4 to 13-Nov, (3.9%) to €175.7 (7 sessions, not yet a swing): D'Ieteren's own and its sector's. On 5-Nov it reversed from €187.0 intraday to close at its low; on 10-Nov it fell 2.5% with the industry up 0.4% and the Stoxx Europe 600 up 0.3%, the day Auto1 fell 4.9%. The cause of either day is not in the sources read.
  • The highs: on closes D'Ieteren's swing highs have fallen at every rally since February: €195.44 (10-Feb), €186.06 (17-Apr), €184.3 (5-Aug), €182.8 (4-Nov). Its lows have risen: €153.86 (30-Mar), €158.90 (8-Jun), €160.0 (24-Sep), €170.4 (28-Oct). The range is narrowing in a triangle, not trending.

The MAs sit in a tight bundle with the slow lines falling: the absolute 200D MA peaked at €173.51 on 28-Oct and has fallen 0.39% to €172.83.

At a flat SP of €175.7 it keeps falling to about €171.8 by mid-December (my calculation), and turns up only once March's lower closes start leaving the window, from about 11-Dec. For the same reason the 150D crosses above the 200D at a flat SP in about eight sessions (my calculation; the daily classification file says about four): a cross made by the 200D falling, not by the SP rising.

On 13-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "D'Ieteren - 200D MA turning negative - Exit?!?" The data confirm it: the absolute 200D MA has fallen since its 28-Oct peak and, on February's arithmetic, will keep falling into December, and the relative 200D MA has fallen (1.35%) over the month. On 10-Nov you wrote: "The stock has breached the 200D MA 4x in 2026 alone". On closes it crossed below its absolute 200D MA nine times in 2026 (my count, 19-Mar to 27-Oct), so your point that a slow MA cannot mark this thesis as broken holds more strongly.

  • The SP above each: partly; 2.9% above the 50D, 1.7% above the absolute 200D, but 0.2% below the 20D and 1.5% below the 10D.
  • Near its 52W high and away from its low: partly; 10.1% below the 52W closing high, 23.1% above the 52W low (€142.7, 25-Nov-2025), short of the 30% the Stage 2 (advancing) test asks.
  • Largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began: fails; 10-Nov's (2.5%) is the largest one-day fall since 24-Sep, and the five sessions to 11-Nov ((3.7%)) the largest five-session fall, though on 0.95x volume and after no good news in the sources read.
  • Wider swings: partly; the average daily range of the last 10 sessions is 2.49% of the close, against 2.02% in the 20 before, still below September's 2.79%.
  • Lower lows: passes; the 13-Nov close is 3.1% above the 28-Oct low (€170.4), but every swing high since February is lower.
  • More down days than up days: fails; 5 of the 7 sessions since 4-Nov fell (one rose, 9-Nov, and one was unchanged, 12-Nov), and 11 of the last 20 against 8 up.
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly; the 4-Nov rise (+3.3%) came on 1.14x and the 5-Nov reversal from €187.0 to a close at the low on 1.49x, the heaviest share volume since 15-Oct.
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: fails; nine closes below the absolute 200D MA in 2026, and on 11 to 13-Nov D'Ieteren traded through the rising 20D MA three days running.

Verdict: neither a Healthy Retest of a Stage 2 (advancing) trend nor yet a broken one, because there is no Stage 2 (advancing) trend to Retest. The rules give D'Ieteren no Stage 2 (advancing) rating and no Stage 3 (topping) rating either (the daily classification file of the 13-Nov close: Stage 2 None, 1 of 4 groups; Stage 3 None, its gate not met); as new capital the same file reads it Inflecting / S1→S2 Probing Bet, with the 150D MA expected to cross above the absolute 200D MA in about four sessions (Probable). The pullback itself is orderly and light-volume. The reading that settles it: the closes of Monday 16 and Tuesday 17-Nov against the 20D MA (about €176.1); a close below €170.4 would make the first lower low since March.

2. Volume, and relative price against its 52W high

D'Ieteren's pullback from its 4-Nov high has come on falling, light volume, while the turn itself came on the heaviest share volume since mid-October: one supply day at the top, then a quiet drift. The 18-Sep option-expiry and index-review day is the only mechanical-volume day in the 50D average; the ratios below leave it out, with the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's in brackets.

  • 5-Nov, the turn: D'Ieteren traded up to €187.0 intraday, above the €186.06 close of 17-Apr that topped its spring rally, then closed at the day's low, €182.3 ((0.3%)), on 91,921 shares (€16.8m), 1.49x the 50D average (1.46x). The script counts it a distribution day. Inter Cars fell 4.2% the same day.
  • 6 to 13-Nov, the drift: 6-Nov (2.0%) on 1.18x (1.15x); 10-Nov (2.5%) on 0.95x (0.93x), an outside day that reached €183.5 and closed at its low; then 11 to 13-Nov on 0.69x, 0.60x and 0.61x (0.67x, 0.58x, 0.59x), 37,000 to 43,000 shares a day (€6.5m to €7.6m).
  • The volume trend: 20D volume stands at 0.89x the 50D average (dashboard), and 12 and 13-Nov traded the fewest shares since 29-Oct.

In the last 25 sessions only one day passed 1.5x on share volume, and it was a sign of supply, not demand. On 15-Oct D'Ieteren traded 102,590 shares (€17.7m), 1.87x (1.81x), closed at the day's low and gained only 0.1% while its industry rose 1.6% and Sixt rose 2.2% on 3.3x. The script labels 15-Oct an accumulation day because the close was up; the close at the low says otherwise. The dashboard's distribution-day count fell from 4 at 16-Oct to 0 at 30-Oct and 1 now (5-Nov); the script also flags 14-Oct.

Up-day against down-day volume has turned against D'Ieteren over both windows the dashboard reads, and the advance itself was bought less heavily than the March to April rally but no more thinly than the two longer advances.

  • 5 and 20 sessions: 0.80 and 0.80 (dashboard); in the 20 sessions there were 11 down days and 8 up days.
  • The whole move: from the 24-Sep low to 13-Nov up/down volume is 1.07, with 4 heavy up days and no heavy down day; the March to April advance (+20.9%) ran at 1.61, the June to August advance (+16.0%) at 1.02, and the November 2025 to February 2026 advance (+37.0%) at 1.07, the same as now. Since 4-Nov the ratio is 0.13 (one up day, 9-Nov, +1.6% on 0.65x).

At the MAs, D'Ieteren has met each line on light volume, which fits an orderly Retest and also a lack of buyers: below the 50D and 150D MAs on 28-Oct at 0.51x, back above the absolute 200D MA on 2-Nov at 0.91x, and below the 20D MA, tranche 1's line, on 13-Nov at 0.61x.

Precedent: D'Ieteren's last decline ended with heavy up days eight and ten sessions after its low. After the 24-Sep low (€160.0) the first came on 6-Oct (+0.9% on 1.6x) and 8-Oct (+2.2% on 2.7x, the Bloomberg News report that Belron had picked four banks for a listing). The 8-Jun low drew no heavy up day in ten sessions and still led to a 16.0% advance, so a missing volume signal has not been decisive for this stock.

Relative price confirmed the SP's lower high: it peaked at 99.45 on 5-Nov, just short of its own falling relative 200D MA (about 99.9), and has fallen 4.5% since to 94.98. Relative price is 19.1% below its 52W high of 2-Feb and 4.4% below its relative 200D MA, below which it has stayed since 6-Aug. The SP's 52W closing high (€195.44, 10-Feb) came with relative price already 7.6% below its own high.

Peers' volume on the same days: Auto1's falls of 6, 10 and 11-Nov came on 1.2x, 1.1x and 1.9x (my calculation from the chart files), heavier than D'Ieteren's; the selling was the sector's, and lighter at D'Ieteren.

Confirms a turn up: a close back above the 20D MA (about €176.1 on Monday 16-Nov), then a close above €182.8 on 1.20x the 50D average or more (about 76,000 shares) in the top quarter of the day's range. Confirms the move continues: a close below the 28-Oct low (€170.4) and the 50D MA (€170.74) on above-average volume.

D'Ieteren's own Stock RS barely moved because its fall (1.6%) was small against the sector's.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts have cut D'Ieteren's EPS for every named FY, and they have cut the nearer years harder, so the earnings recovery is being pushed out rather than raised.

  • FY2026 EPS: €12.75, (2.4%) since 30-Sep, (4.8%) over 3 months, (13.8%) over 12 months.
  • FY2027 EPS: €15.09, (1.7%), (3.5%) and (12.5%).
  • FY2028 EPS: €17.38, (1.9%), (3.8%) and (7.2%).
  • Implied growth: FY2026 to FY2027 now 18.4%, from 16.7% three months ago and 16.6% twelve months ago; FY2027 to FY2028 15.2%, from 15.5% and 8.6%. The higher growth comes from a lower FY2026 base, not higher later years.

Revenue and EBITDA were cut in September and have not moved since; EPS was cut again on 1 and 26-Oct.

  • Revenue: FY2027 €8,275m, (2.0%) over 3 months, unchanged since 24-Sep.
  • EBITDA: FY2027 €686.9m, (4.2%) over 3 months, unchanged since 30-Sep. The implied FY2026 EBITDA margin fell to 7.9% from 8.2% three months ago, and FY2027's to 8.3% from 8.5%: analysts cut margins more than sales.
  • EPS, day by day (FY2026): cut 3.4% on 10-Sep, the day after the half-year results, and 1.7% on 1-Oct; raised 0.9% on 15-Oct; cut 1.6% on 26-Oct (FY2028 (2.2%)).

The SP followed the estimates on the two biggest cuts and ignored them in between. D'Ieteren fell 3.4% on 4.4x on 10-Sep with the first cut, and 1.8% on 27-Oct, the session after the 26-Oct cut, against an industry up 0.1% (cause not in the sources read; hypothesis: the 26-Oct cut). The 28-Oct to 4-Nov rally of 7.3% came on unchanged estimates, which fits item 3 (RS)'s reading that it was the industry's rotation. Hypothesis: the cuts are D'Ieteren Automotive's (first-half adjusted profit before tax (66.6%), 51% below consensus, earnings review of 9-Sep), with Belron's 28.6% growth limiting them. Test: the next change in the daily series, and whether it comes with Belron news.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

D'Ieteren's ratings carry no signal: all 14 analysts rate it Buy at 13-Nov, up from 12 of 12 in August, through a 13.2% fall to 24-Sep and every cut in item 4 (Consensus by named FY). Coverage has grown from 7 analysts in late 2024 to 14, and every new analyst started at Buy, so the rating count follows neither valuation nor momentum.

D'Ieteren's analysts are trimming PTs while cutting revenue and EBITDA, and the PT breadth that held the case up is narrowing.

  • Average PT: €234.7 since 28-Oct, from €239.5 on 13-Oct ((2.0%)); it stands 33.6% above the SP, against 38.1% on 27-Oct and 30.8% at the August month-end.
  • PT Net Upgrade Breadth: +29% at 13-Nov (5 raising, 1 cutting, of 14), from +36% at the October month-end and +46% at September's.
  • Revenue Net Upgrade Breadth: (56%) (5 of 9 cutting, none raising), the SS Sales reading of the Net Upgrade Breadth Sell Criterion; EBITDA: (62%) (5 of 8 cutting); EPS: (21%) (4 of 14 cutting, 1 raising). Each has been negative at every month-end since September.

Who is cutting, and why: the brokers behind the October PT cuts and the revenue cuts are not named in the sources read (no AlphaSense report this run). The earnings review of 9-Sep names Deutsche Bank cutting its profit before tax forecast by 1% for the heavier drag from D'Ieteren Automotive; Jefferies Research, cited in the key-questions research filed on 31-Oct-2026, warned that D'Ieteren's PT still assumes a 30% holding discount unless a Belron listing is followed by substantial share buybacks. On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Upgrade breadth is very negative; maybe due to accounting conventions; IA to check." The data say the negative breadth is in revenue and EBITDA, the two lines D'Ieteren Automotive drives, while PT breadth stays positive: that pattern fits real car-arm cuts with Belron holding the PTs, more than an accounting convention. The check you asked for is not yet filed.

Precedent: the same divergence ran at the March and April 2026 month-ends (PT breadth +30% and +18%, revenue breadth (25%) and (60%)), across the 20.9% rally that ended on 17-Apr; the SP gave back most of it by 8-Jun. Test: a PT cut without Belron news would show the PT support itself giving way.

9. Peers

D'Ieteren's cohort holds no other stock, so its peers are its sector's seven, as in the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's table.

D'Ieteren's sector is still split in two, and this week the broken half pulled the sector's RS down with D'Ieteren. On the dashboard's peer table, Inter Cars, Sixt, ZIGUP and Bilia hold above their absolute 200D MAs with positive revenue Net Upgrade Breadth; Auto1 ((14.1%) in the week to 13-Nov, Stock RS 3), Inchcape and Ayvens trade below theirs. D'Ieteren sits just above its slow MAs, but its revenue Net Upgrade Breadth, (56%), is the weakest in the sector after Auto1's (73%).

Your four peer checks, for the week to 13-Nov:

  • Sell-side estimates: the split runs through the 3-month EPS revisions: Inter Cars +9%, Bilia +1%, Sixt 0%; D'Ieteren (4%), Ayvens (4%), Inchcape (9%), Auto1 (11%). D'Ieteren's cuts match the car retailers', not the parts and rental names'.
  • SP technicals: Auto1 fell on 6, 10 and 11-Nov (6.7%, 4.9%, 5.3%), and D'Ieteren's two largest one-day falls since its 24-Sep low, 6 and 10-Nov, came on two of those three days.
  • Newsflow: Sixt reported its third quarter on 12-Nov and rose 1.7% on 3.4x, then fell 2.4% on 1.6x on 13-Nov; what it reported is not in the sources read. The cause of Auto1's fall is not in the sources read.
  • Company guidance: Inchcape cut its FY2026 adjusted operating margin guidance to about 5.5% from about 6% on 22-Oct (Hargreaves Lansdown, 22-Oct, in the 28-Oct note); D'Ieteren kept its FY2026 guidance of low-to-mid-single-digit growth in adjusted profit before tax on 9-Sep (earnings review).

Hypothesis, ranked first: D'Ieteren now trades with its car-retail half on bad days and with its industry on good days. For it: 6 and 10-Nov with Auto1; the revenue and EBITDA cuts of item 4 (Consensus by named FY) that fit D'Ieteren Automotive; the 28-Oct to 4-Nov rally that came with Consumer discretionary's rotation. Against it: D'Ieteren's daily moves correlate most with Sixt (0.31, moderate), a rental company, and only weakly with Auto1 (0.23); and on 22-Oct D'Ieteren fell only 0.4% when Inchcape fell 9.3%. Test: the next day Auto1 or Inchcape falls 4% or more without Belron news; a D'Ieteren fall of 2% or more with it would confirm.

Who turned first: no one; Inter Cars rose 4.8% on 4-Nov and fell 4.2% on 5-Nov, the day D'Ieteren reversed from €187.0, and Auto1 turned on 6-Nov.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "InterCars has strong SS momentum; maybe more interesting than D'Ieteren. Investigate". The readings support the comparison: Inter Cars carries the sector's only clearly rising estimates (3-month EPS +9%, revenue +3%), Stock RS of 96 for three months, and an SP 1.6% below its 52W high; D'Ieteren has none of the three. Inter Cars is also the parts business inside the "older cars, more repairs" story that is D'Ieteren's own strongest theme through Belron (Sector Digest, 10-Nov).

What the peers imply for D'Ieteren's sell tests: with Auto1, Inchcape and Ayvens at Stock RS 21 or below, Sector RS needs the holding-up half to rise to clear 50 by 30-Nov; D'Ieteren's own move matters little to that test.

Richard decides.

Bally's Intralot's SP has gone nowhere for a year, and the nine-month results that made full-year guidance a stretch did not move it.   • 10-Nov, preliminary nine-month results: third-quarter adjusted EBITDA €88.9m on revenue of €286.1m, a 31.1% margin against 36.5% a year earlier; UK constant-currency online revenue +12.2%; 48.5% of the €68.0m gross UK duty hit offset, against the "approximately two-thirds" management had described; pro forma leverage 4.33 times, from 4.05 at June. Why a weak print left the SP flat, ranked by evidence.

  • (1) Holders price Evoke and flows, not standalone guidance (the earnings summary's "reading three"): no move on 10 and 11-Nov. Against: untested until guidance is cut.

Against: 13-Nov rose as the parent fell.
  • What would settle it: the move on the 26-Nov release; under (1), a guidance cut passes inside the usual weekly 3.5%.

On 10-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Are SS EPS downgrades partly technical, accounting related?… IA to investigate…". The Investment Analyst holds the task; our files say mostly yes, with a real financing part beneath.

  • For a technical change: a one-step cut on the day the analyst set changed, while FY2026 EBITDA fell 2.7%; the question list (12-Nov) puts the only visible broker EPS (Barclays: €0.020, €0.030, €0.040) near the reported basis, with "a gap of EUR 70m to 80m a year between adjusted and reported profit"; the earnings review records €66.58m of purchase-price-allocation amortisation, cutting pro forma net profit by €44.22m over 12 months, after restating the acquired interactive business to IFRS.
  • Against: €1.63bn of adjusted net debt (about €140m of interest, the Investment Analyst's model) and leverage up to 4.33 times; adjusted pre-tax profit is about 30% of EBITDA, so each 1% off EBITDA takes 3.6% off adjusted net income.
  • So: the EPS line is one broker's reported-basis figure with little signal; FY2026 EBITDA against guidance is the line that will move.

The links. The SP neither led nor followed: flat on 30-Sep, +4.6% from 8 to 21-Oct with no estimate change. The Investment Analyst puts 65% on a guidance cut or reframe (Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 9-Nov). Test: FY2026 EBITDA consensus after the 26-Nov release; a cut towards the nine-month run-rate would confirm the earnings summary's "reading one", that the margin, not volume, is the problem.

The three findings that matter most:

  • The results moved the case, not the SP: a 31.1% third-quarter margin, leverage of 4.33 times and a fourth quarter that must be 67% above the third for guidance; FY2026 EBITDA consensus (€408.5m) still implies a fourth-quarter margin near 45%. The 26-Nov release tests the gap.

1. SP and MAs

Hexatronic's bounce from the 6-Nov low failed its first test: up 11.9% in three sessions to 40.74 on 11-Nov, then down 6.6% in two sessions to 38.05 on 13-Nov, 0.8% above its rising absolute 200D MA. Over the week to 13-Nov Hexatronic rose 4.5% against the Stoxx Europe 600's 2.4% and Technology's 2.6%; beta (1.15) explains 2.8 points, leaving a stock-specific 1.7 points, 0.2 times its usual weekly move of 8.8%. Hexatronic is down 19.5% from its 29-Oct closing high (47.28), 5.7% over 3 months (Technology (0.6%)) and up 115.2% over 12 months.

This week's two legs were the fibre and optical group's, with Hexatronic at the volatile end of both.

  • 6 to 11-Nov, 36.41 to 40.74, +11.9%: Nokia +11.0%, Prysmian +10.7%, Mycronic +8.5%, Nexans +7.6% (chart files); on 11-Nov Technology rose 2.1% and the Stoxx Europe 600 0.7%
  • 11 to 13-Nov, 40.74 to 38.05, (6.6%): Nokia (5.9%), Mycronic (4.1%), Prysmian (3.7%), Nexans (2.7%); on 13-Nov the Stoxx Europe 600 was flat, so that day was the group's
  • The cause of either leg is not in the sources read. Richard's weekly AlphaSense report was not read this run (the AlphaSense sign-in form showed, and no Chrome browser was connected), and two web searches on 17-Nov found no Hexatronic news dated 11 to 13-Nov

This week favours last week's stronger hypothesis for the 29-Oct to 6-Nov fall, a fibre-group de-rating on unchanged numbers, over holders selling: the group moved together and Hexatronic's down days were light. What would reverse it: a heavy-volume Hexatronic fall on a day the group rises.

Hexatronic's MAs: the SP regained the 50D, 100D and 150D on 11-Nov and lost all three on 12-Nov; it now sits between the falling short MAs and the rising absolute 200D MA.

  • The close is 4.5% to 8.1% below the 20D, 50D, 100D and 150D; the 100D is falling and the 50D, flat over 5 sessions, is 0.5% above the 150D (12.6% in mid-August)
  • The calendar: at a flat SP the rising absolute 200D MA reaches 38.08 on Friday 20-Nov and about 38.39 at the 30-Nov month-end (my projection from the chart file): Hexatronic must rise just to stay above it
  • At earlier turns the lows from mid-September held near the rising 150D MA (18-Sep 1.2% below it, 28-Sep 0.4% above, 20-Oct 1.6% below), while the 29-Jul low sat 7.1% above it; the 6-Nov low broke the 150D and closed 2.5% below the absolute 200D MA, one line lower, before the 10-Nov close regained it

On 13-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Very positive OBV but SP pulled back to 200D MA; if it hits the 150D and doesn't pierce it, big problem… Major risk…!" Hexatronic's SP closed 0.9% above the 150D MA on 11-Nov (40.74 against 40.36), back below it on 12-Nov on 0.74 times the 50D average volume, and 5.8% below it on 13-Nov. One close through and no hold: nearer your "big problem" case than a regain. Your note's last sentence ("Did I do the SP") is cut off, so I cannot answer it.

On 12-Nov you wrote: "NB: VCP could actually be Lower Lows - Major Red Flag", and "Nov-26 Rebound very Tennis Ball-like".

  • The pullbacks contracted, then broke: 20.3% (July), 10.8% (to 18-Sep), 9.5% (to 20-Oct), then 23.0% (to 6-Nov), the deepest since June
  • The lows: 36.41 (6-Nov) is below the 18-Sep, 28-Sep and 20-Oct lows and 0.6% above the 29-Jul low (36.19): lower lows against the last three, not yet against July
  • The tennis ball: short and aggressive (+11.9% in three sessions), but 62% of it given back in two

Healthy Retest or broken trend? Hexatronic against your trend-health tests

  • The absolute 200D MA rising: passes; +5.1% over 20 sessions, +1.0% over 5
  • The 150D above the absolute 200D MA, and the 50D above both: passes, the second narrowly (the 50D 0.5% above the 150D)
  • The SP above each MA: fails; 4.5% to 8.1% below the 20D, 50D, 100D and 150D; 0.8% above the absolute 200D
  • Near its 52W high: fails; 21.7% below 48.58
  • Away from its low: passes; 127.8% above the 16.70 low (18-Nov-2025)
  • The largest one-day or one-week fall since the advance began, especially after good news: fails; the 20.5% fall in the week to 6-Nov is the largest calendar-week fall since 18-Nov-2025, eleven sessions after the 22-Oct beat
  • Wider swings: fails; average true range 6.23% over the last month against 4.69% over three months (data file)
  • Lower lows: partly; below the last three lows, above the 29-Jul low
  • More down days than up days: partly; 6 of the last 10 sessions down, 9 of the last 20
  • A light-volume breakout reversed on heavy volume: partly; the breakout was heavy (5.62 times, 22-Oct), and so was the reversal (1.86 to 2.19 times)
  • The SP tracking a rising MA instead of bouncing off it: partly; a crisp bounce from 2.5% below the absolute 200D MA, then a light-volume drift back to 0.8% above it
  • Verdict: the short-term trend is broken and the long-term trend is under test at the absolute 200D MA, with this week's failed hold of the 150D a mark against it. The daily classification file of the 13-Nov close rates Hexatronic's Stage 3 (topping) Plausible (3 of its 6 tests passed: the 50D below 103% of the 150D, range expansion of 1.10 or more, two or more lower lows in two months), beside a Stage 2 (advancing) rating of only Plausible (5 of 7 tests, failing the price above the 150D and the price above the 50D): the system's own topping reading now stands next to the advancing one. What settles it: a close above 40.74 (11-Nov) on 2.17m shares or more (1.5 times the 50D average), in the top third of the range, makes it a Retest that held; a close below 36.41, or a confirmed exit under the 200-day line rule, makes it a broken trend

Ratios are the volume evidence script's, without the 18-Sep mechanical-volume day; the PMS Per-stock Dashboard's figure follows in brackets where it differs.

Hexatronic's bounce and its give-back both point the right way on volume: the rally came on above-average volume, and the two down days on below-average volume.

  • 11-Nov: +6.4% on 1.90m shares (SEK 77.2m), 1.32 times the 50D average, a 1.5% gap up and a close at the day's high: an accumulation day, but short of the 1.5 times (2.12m shares) set last week as the test of a turn
  • 12 and 13-Nov: (3.3%) on 1.06m shares (SEK 41.9m, 0.74 times) and (3.4%) on 1.24m (SEK 47.4m, 0.86 times), each gapping down and closing in the bottom third of the range: not distribution days, but sellers still set the close

The heavy-volume days of the last 25 sessions (1.5 times the 50D average or more) are the late-October advance (22, 23, 26 and 28-Oct, 1.67 to 5.62 times), 15-Oct ((3.4%) on 2.29 times), the 4 to 6-Nov fall (SEK 316.5m traded on 1.86 to 2.19 times, each day closing in the bottom 4% of its range) and 9-Nov (+2.7% on 1.57 times); none since.

Distribution has stopped growing and starts to drop out of the count next week. The volume evidence script flags five distribution days in 25 sessions (15 and 19-Oct; 4, 5 and 6-Nov) against eight accumulation days (21, 22, 23, 26, 28 and 29-Oct; 9 and 11-Nov). The dashboard counts four, from one at 30-Oct; 15 and 19-Oct leave the window by about 23-Nov.

Up-day against down-day volume: positive over 5 and 20 sessions, negative over the move from the 29-Oct high.

  • The dashboard's 5-session ratio is 1.57 (0.66 on 10-Nov) and its 20-session ratio 1.38. Since the 6-Nov low up-day volume is 2.50 times down-day volume (5.77m against 2.31m shares; my count)

On 12-Nov you wrote in your Notion note: "Hugely positive OBV over last few months… Very positive IMHO". The chart file supports you: on-balance volume rose 19.4m shares from 20 to 29-Oct and has given back only 5.9m (30%), while the SP gave back all of its gain, so on-balance volume stands 13.6m shares above its 20-Oct level with the SP 1.8% below (my count). The caveat: the 22-Oct report day counts 6.18m shares as buying, yet closed in the bottom quarter of its range.

Volume on the MAs: the breaks were heavy (5-Nov, 2.06 times; 6-Nov, 2.19 times), the regains lighter (10-Nov, 1.18 times; 11-Nov, 1.32 times) and the 12-Nov loss of the 50D to 150D light (0.74 times). The 200-day line rule counts from 11-Nov, so the 6 and 9-Nov closes below the line fall before its start.

Precedent: three of Hexatronic's declines ended with a heavy up day (2.5 to 4.0 times) within six sessions of the low; after the 29-Jul low no day passed 1.3 times and nearly three months of range followed before the late-October breakout, the whole move from the 29-Jul low (36.19) to the 29-Oct high (47.28) a 30.6% rise. Five sessions after 6-Nov the heaviest up day is 1.57 times: more like 29-Jul, with no report before 4-Feb-2027.

Hexatronic's relative price confirmed the SP's low and is 26.5% below its 52W high (3-Jun), 1.5 points beyond the 25% line that Sell Criterion reads at the month-end.

  • 28.5% below on 6-Nov, 21.5% on 11-Nov and 24.0% on 12-Nov (inside the line for two sessions), 26.5% on 13-Nov; relative price is 6.0% below its relative 200D MA after one close above it (11-Nov), and its 6-Nov close (19.98) undercut the 29-Jul low (20.17)
  • The month-end: at a flat market Hexatronic needs a 30-Nov close at or above 38.84, 2.1% above the 13-Nov close, to stay inside the line (data file). Since June relative price has closed beyond the line on 21 days and was back inside it at every month-end, at 31-Jul by 0.4 points
  • On 10-Nov you wrote: "Hexatronic + Avanza very near 30% below 52W High". On the Sell Criterion's basis (relative price, 25%), Hexatronic is already beyond the line; on the SP it is 21.7% below its 52W closing high

Peers' volume: the group's 11-Nov rally was light (Nokia 0.68 times) except Nexans (1.72) and Hexatronic (1.32), and its 12 and 13-Nov falls were mostly light (Nokia 0.66 and 0.72 times, Prysmian 0.66 and 0.59), the exception Nexans, which fell (1.7%) and (1.0%) on above-average volume (1.21 and 1.28 times) (chart files).

3. RS

Hexatronic's Stock RS recovered to 86 at the 13-Nov Friday reading from 76 on 6-Nov, 16 points above the 70 line; Hardware's Sector RS stays near the bottom and Technology's Industry RS has not moved.

  • Stock RS 86 (13-Nov), 76 (6-Nov), 92 (4-Nov), 96 (30-Oct), 97 (9-Oct); 94 a month ago and 95 three months ago. 76 was the lowest reading since 20-Feb-2026 (70); the 27-Feb month-end read 77
  • Hardware's Sector RS 16 (13-Nov), from 7 (6-Nov), 26 (30-Oct) and 64 (2-Oct); below 50 at every reading since 14-Oct (49)
  • Technology's Industry RS 59 at both November Fridays, from 79 (30-Oct) and 86 at every Friday from 25-Sep to 23-Oct; 29 points above the 30 line

The order of the turn was the sector first (from 2-Oct), the industry second (from 30-Oct) and the stock last (from 4-Nov); the stock is first back, with Industry RS flat at 59. Hypothesis: the week's rebound was the fibre group's (item 1 (SP and MAs)), not a rotation back into Technology, so Hexatronic's Stock RS can fall again with the group without any help from the industry.

Hexatronic is its sector's strongest stock by a wide margin, as in August: Ericsson 54, secunet Security Networks 48 (94 a month ago), Kitron 42 (90), Nokia 24 (88) (data file). On 21-Aug Hardware's Sector RS was 17 and Hexatronic's Stock RS 94, the same split, and Hexatronic rose 20.8% from its 21-Aug close (39.14) to 29-Oct.

At 30-Nov the Sector RS with Stock RS test turns on Hexatronic's Stock RS alone (Hardware is 34 points below 50). Confirms the turn down: Stock RS below 70 at 30-Nov. Refutes it: Stock RS back above 90 while Hardware stays low, as in August.

4. Consensus by named FY

No Hexatronic estimate has moved since 27 and 28-Oct, while the SP has fallen 19.5% from its 29-Oct high: the SP is moving first, as your 3-Nov note expects ("Almost always, price moves first").

  • One month (30-Sep to 13-Nov): EPS FY2026 +7.2%, FY2027 +10.3%, FY2028 +7.0%; revenue +3.3%, +3.8%, +3.2%; EBITDA +5.1%, +4.5%, +3.5% (five analysts)
  • Six months: EPS +12.4%, +14.6%, +13.9%; your "upgrading over N3-6M" rule is met

The 22-Oct report pulled earnings forward rather than raising the trajectory. FY2027 EPS rose faster than FY2028 over the month (10.3% against 7.0%), so the implied FY2027 to FY2028 EPS growth fell to 11.5% from 15.0% a month ago; FY2026 to FY2027 is 21.6%. Implied revenue growth slows from 10.4% to 5.7%, and the implied EBITDA margin edges up (13.4%, 14.0%, 14.3%); FY2027 EPS rose more than twice as fast as FY2027 EBITDA (10.3% against 4.5%), the extra from below the EBITDA line (adjusted leverage down to 1.3 times; earnings review, 22-Oct).

Consensus now sits above the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's own view, with nothing scheduled to support it before 4-Feb-2027. FY2027 EPS of SEK 2.946 is 6.0% above the memo's SEK 2.78 (consensus basis). The counter-case: the memo expected this gap; it forecast that FactSet would absorb Redeye's post-Q3 update (SEK 2.85 for FY2027) in November as "a +5-9% revision burst that is the Q3 print being recorded, not new information", expected FY2027 consensus at end-April 2027 "within ±5% of SEK 2.85" (SEK 2.946 is 3.4% above 2.85, inside that band), and puts about 70% on a beat of the four-contributor bar at the February 2027 report. The Sellside Analysts' Memo (3-Nov) reports the Investment Analyst's judgement that the upgrade cycle is over, and his chance of a cut at "about 40% at each of the next two reports" from mid-2027. Management warned on 22-Oct that Data Center would normalise in the fourth quarter and that glass fibre and some connectors were restricting growth (earnings review, 22-Oct). Test: the year-end report of 4-Feb-2027, against both warnings.

No analyst is cutting Hexatronic: four Buys of four, an average PT of SEK 50.2 unchanged since 2-Nov (SEK 49.75 from 23-Oct), and Net Upgrade Breadth of +100% on EPS, revenue, EBITDA and PT (five of five raising within the 100-day window, none cutting).

  • Ratings went from three Buys and one other (30-Sep) to four Buys of four (October month-end), and the average PT from SEK 47.60 to 50.20 (Finwire's consensus summary via Placera, 6-Nov)
  • Latest named PT changes in the sources read: SB1 Markets SEK 50 to 52 (29-Oct); Redeye's Base Case SEK 44 to 46 (23-Oct) (Finwire via Börsvärlden, read in the 6-Nov run)
  • The breadth is stale, not fresh: every raise sits in the 22-Oct to 2-Nov round (the last, the average PT's step to SEK 50.2 on 2-Nov); no estimate has changed since 28-Oct. On five analysts, one cut moves revenue or PT breadth from +100% to +60%

Hexatronic's average PT sits 31.9% above its SP, from 5.2% at the 29-Oct high (on that day's average PT of SEK 49.75) and 37.9% on 6-Nov: analysts have not followed the SP down. The last time the gap stood above 30% (September-2024 to June-2025 month-ends) it closed by a 40% PT cut and the loss of every Buy; in July 2026 it reached 25.7% and the SP then rose 30.6%.

Hypothesis: Hexatronic's analysts rate on results, not on price (Buys added after the 5-Feb, 15-Jul and 22-Oct-2026 reports; against, a Buy dropped in March and in June without a report), so their silence says little before 4-Feb-2027. What would change this reading: a PT cut or a rating change from Redeye or SB1 Markets before the 30-Nov month-end, when the Net Upgrade Breadth with Stock RS test is read; it is far (Stock RS 86, breadth +100%).

What the peers imply for Hexatronic: the group's estimates give no reason to expect Hexatronic's to be cut, so the 30-Nov sell tests turn on sentiment and price; the group's mostly light-volume fall of 12 and 13-Nov (Nexans the exception) reads as a pause, and no capitulation day like Corning's 28-Jul fall, which ended July's decline, has come.

What the rules say for Hexatronic now, and what decides the next step

  • The long-term trend holds by 0.8%, and the line is rising towards the SP: the bounce held the 150D MA for one session; at a flat SP the rising absolute 200D MA catches the SP on about Friday 20-Nov (item 1 (SP and MAs))

  • All four tranches (4.65% of capital): hold. No Sell Criterion gives a sell signal at its reading day, and the 200-day line rule is clear (the line 37.75, the stop-loss 35.49, the close 38.05). Nothing sells at the Tuesday 17-Nov session
  • The nearest sell test, relative price more than 25% below its 52W high (read at the month-end): 26.5% below today, beyond the line. Its pace: 28.5% a week before (6-Nov), 20.6% four weeks before (16-Oct), 20.5% thirteen weeks before (18-Aug). A close below it gives a sell signal that sells the whole holding at the Tuesday 1-Dec session

  • How the tests interact: at 30-Nov the projected line (about 38.39) sits 1.2% below the relative-price level (38.84). 11. Carlsberg against each Sell Criterion (readings of 2026-11-13)

IMCD's results left consensus EPS where it was and trimmed EBITDA: FY2027 EPS is up 0.1% since 4-Nov, FY2027 EBITDA down 0.7%.

  • EPS: FY2026 €5.634, FY2027 €6.073, FY2028 €6.625: each up 0.0% to 0.1% over one month, 0.9% to 1.6% over three, 5.2% to 5.8% over six, and down 6.1%, 8.2% and 15.4% over twelve. All three rose about 0.7% on 5-Nov and gave it back on 6-Nov
  • EBITDA: rose about 1.0% on 5-Nov, then was cut 1.7% to 1.9% on 9-Nov for FY2026 and FY2028, and 1.6% over 6 and 9-Nov for FY2027 (€625.5m to €619.4m on 6-Nov, to €615.4m on 9-Nov); against 4-Nov the three years are down 0.7% to 0.8%

  • The trajectory: implied growth FY2026 to FY2027 is 7.8% for EPS and 4.1% for revenue; FY2027 to FY2028, 9.1% and 5.0%. The implied EBITDA margin rises 0.1 point a year (11.3%, 11.4%, 11.5%); no year is being raised faster than another
  • The link: the cuts were to EBITDA and revenue, the lines the conversion miss and the EMEA slowdown touch; EPS held, possibly because lower net debt (leverage 2.6 times EBITDA at 30-Sep, from 2.8 times at December 2025; earnings review) offsets the EBITDA trim below the operating line. The Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo's three tests for the results all passed, the conversion margin by 0.1 point: organic gross profit growth 9.6% (line: zero), gross margin 25.1% (24.5%), conversion margin 42.1% (42%) (earnings review, 5-Nov). 5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Two Buys went in the days after the 5-Nov results, on 9 and 10-Nov in the data file's daily ratings, not on the day of the 13-Nov high: 9 Buys, 4 others and 1 Sell among 14 analysts, from 11, 3 and 1 among 15 on 5-Nov. One was Jefferies, which downgraded IMCD to Hold from Buy on 11-Nov and cut its PT to €115 from €122, concluding that "the year-to-date share price recovery of 32% leaves limited room for further valuation expansion" (Jefferies, 11-Nov, "Spec Chem Distributors: Inflation Fades, Waiting for Volumes; Downgrade to Hold", in the earnings review). Hypothesis: IMCD's analysts move their ratings with the gap to their PTs, not with the SP's momentum. For it: Buys peaked at 14 in September and October 2025 with the average PT 65% and 56% above the SP; they fell to 9 at the April 2026 month-end with the gap at 12.2%, and fell to 9 again now with the gap at 10.2%; Jefferies' stated reason for its 11-Nov downgrade is valuation after the SP's recovery. Against it: Buys rose from 10 to 11 in July while the SP rose 20%. The second Buy withdrawn (9 or 10-Nov) is not named in the sources read (your AlphaSense report could not be read).

IMCD's average PT (€116.9) is 10.2% above the SP, the narrowest gap at any month-end since September 2024 (6.5%). It fell 35% from March 2025 to March 2026, reached €118.6 on 6-Nov and eased; it is up 0.8% since 4-Nov. The PT actions named in the earnings review, after the 5-Nov results: Jefferies unchanged on the day (Buy, €122), then downgraded to Hold with a PT of €115 on 11-Nov; Deutsche Bank Buy, €123, maintained; Morgan Stanley Equal-weight, €110 (€105 on 29-Jul, earnings review of 10-Aug). Four days after the April month-end gap of 12.2% came the 4-May top and a 21.9% fall to 30-Jun.

What the rules say for IMCD now, and what decides the next step

The three findings that matter most for your decision:

4. Consensus by named FY

FY2027 is CVS Group's year to June 2027; figures are FactSet consensus in sterling.

CVS Group's consensus turned up after the results, and the furthest year rose fastest: analysts are lifting the trajectory a little, not pulling earnings forward.

  • EPS: FY2027 95.6p, +1.1% in a month (from the 30-Sep month-end), flat over 3 months, +0.7% over 6; FY2028 104.1p, +0.9%, (0.7%) and (0.6%); FY2029 113.1p, +1.6%, (3.4%) and (1.2%).
  • Revenue: FY2027 £751.0m, +0.4% in a month; FY2028 £785.3m, +0.6%; FY2029 £823.8m, +2.1%.
  • EBITDA: FY2027 £150.8m, +0.3%; FY2028 £159.1m, +0.6%; FY2029 £167.5m, +1.1%, still (3.7%) over 6 months.
  • The turning point: FY2027 EPS bottomed at 94.5p on 1-Oct, a week after the results, then rose on 8-Oct (+0.7%) and on 3 and 4-Nov (+0.5%); FY2029 revenue rose 1.0% on each occasion.

The implied growth is steady at about 9% a year in EPS and 5% in revenue and EBITDA, and the implied margin barely moves.

  • EPS grows 11.7% from FY2026's reported 85.6p to FY2027, then 8.8% to FY2028 and 8.6% to FY2029; revenue 5.4%, 4.6% and 4.9%; EBITDA 6.6%, 5.5% and 5.3%.
  • Implied EBITDA margin: 20.1% for FY2027, 20.3% for FY2028 and FY2029, against 19.9% reported for FY2026, near the bottom of the company's 19% to 23% target range.
  • EPS growing faster than EBITDA fits the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo (4-Nov): the buyback and acquisitions make FY2027's EPS growth "close to arithmetic before any organic recovery".

The SP led and the estimates followed only a little: the move is a re-rating on almost unchanged numbers. From the 1-Oct trough to 13-Nov, FY2027 EPS rose 1.2% while the SP rose 21.7% (1,159.9p to 1,412p). The test is organic growth: UK like-for-like growth slowed to about 1.5% in the second half of FY2026 from 2.7% in the first (earnings review, 24-Sep). The four-month trading update comes with the annual general meeting in late November (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 4-Nov; the date is not in the sources read). The memo's test: "A November update that reports group LFL above 2.5 percent (which would mean practice LFL near 2 percent) would move the FY2027 number toward 96p and the breadth of revisions to neutral four months early." The memo's 96p is its own FY2027 estimate, not consensus. FY2027 consensus is already 95.6p, so that outcome would lift the memo's estimate to about where analysts already are rather than raise consensus much. Its larger effect would be on the breadth of revisions, now (10%) for FY2027 EPS. Confirms: group like-for-like growth above 2.5%. Refutes: a figure near the second half's 1.5%.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

CVS Group's SP has run almost to its average PT: the gap is 13.7%, the narrowest month-end or latest reading in the 24 months on file, as the PT is cut and the SP rises.

  • Average PT 1,605p on 13-Nov, cut 2.5% on 3-Nov from 1,645p (after a one-day rise from 1,626p on 2-Nov), and down 2.1% since the September month-end (1,640p), while the SP rose 15.8% from 1,218.9p.
  • The gap was 34.5% at the September month-end and 22.5% at the October month-end; the previous narrowest was 21.0% at the May 2025 month-end.
  • Jefferies' Hold and 1,400p PT (earnings review, 24-Sep) is now below the SP; Deutsche Bank's Buy and 1,700p PT (the same source) is 20.4% above it.

Ratings follow valuation, not momentum: analysts add Buys after falls and drop them near highs.

  • Buys fell from 9 of 11 to 8 of 11 on 13-Nov, the day CVS Group stood 0.3% below its 52W closing high; the broker is not named in the sources read.
  • The same happened at the last high: Buys fell from 8 to 7 at the February 2026 month-end. After the 23-Jun-2026 low they rose from 5 to 9.

Net Upgrade Breadth diverges: analysts are raising revenue and cutting the PT, as they were in the 28-Oct note, and the PT side is getting worse.

  • PT breadth (27%) on 13-Nov (0 raised, 3 cut, of 11), from (18%) at 30-Oct and 0% at 30-Sep: the third cut came in November.
  • Revenue breadth +33% (3 raised, none cut, of 9), unchanged from 30-Oct; EBITDA +11% (1 raised of 9); EPS (10%) (1 raised, 2 cut, of 10).
  • Who is cutting, and why: the brokers cutting PTs are not named in the sources read, since your weekly AlphaSense report was not read this week. The one stated logic on file is Jefferies' at the results: a valuation built on "a standalone discounted cash flow model that purposely excludes any future earnings contribution from mergers and acquisitions" (earnings review, 24-Sep).
  • Hypothesis: brokers are cutting what they pay for a slower organic path while acquisitions lift revenue. For: revenue breadth positive, EPS breadth negative, the second half's like-for-like slowdown. Against: FY2027 EPS rose on 3 and 4-Nov, beside the 3-Nov PT cut. Test: the late-November trading update.

9. Peers

CVS Group trades with the companion-animal businesses, not with its own cohort of human-healthcare clinics.

  • From the 2-Oct low to 13-Nov, CVS Group rose 23.9%, Pets at Home (the UK pet retailer and vet group, outside the cohort) 27.7% and Vimian (the Swedish animal health group) 20.6%. The cohort barely moved or fell: Medicover +2.8%, Spire Healthcare +0.2%, Diagnostyka (1.9%), Aevis Victoria (6.3%), Terveystalo (13.4%).
  • Over 63 sessions CVS Group's daily moves correlate 0.37 with Pets at Home's and 0.39 with Vimian's, against (0.03) to 0.09 with Medicover, Spire Healthcare and Terveystalo (raw daily moves, market included; my calculation).
  • On 4-Nov, CVS Group's heaviest day of the advance (2.20 times the 50D average), Pets at Home rose 1.6% and Vimian 2.3%, each on 1.8 times its own average; on 21-Oct, CVS Group's first distribution day, Pets at Home fell 1.5% on 2.3 times.
  • Hypothesis: CVS Group's advance is a UK and European companion-animal re-rating after the Competition and Markets Authority's final order of 22-Sep, which the earnings review described as "focusing on transparency rather than structural break-ups or vertical divestments". For: the three move together, and Vimian reported organic growth of 11% in its third quarter (earnings review). Against: neither is in CVS Group's cohort on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard, so their estimates are not on the page. Test: Pets at Home's interim results on 25-Nov (the Stock-specific Investment Case A&J Memo, 4-Nov).

The human-healthcare cohort is weakening, and it pulled the Sector RS down this week. Medicover's Stock RS fell from 78 to 71 in a month and its SP 2.4%; Spire Healthcare's fell from 84 to 61, its SP pinned near the 250p cash offer; Aevis Victoria's from 68 to 54. Terveystalo (Stock RS 5) cut its 2026 adjusted operating profit guidance to €110m to €120m on 20-Oct and is 18.9% below its absolute 200D MA. In the week Fresenius Medical Care fell 5.0%, Ramsay Santé 4.8% and Medicover 2.2%, while the Nordic care leaders rose (Ambea +1.9%, Stock RS 97; Attendo +2.4%, Stock RS 90).

Your four peer checks, in short:

  • Sell-side estimates: the cohort is being cut over 3 months (Medicover revenue (10%), Terveystalo EPS (17%), Aevis Victoria EPS (5%)), while CVS Group's are flat to up; only Spire Healthcare's EPS rises (+35%).
  • SP technicals: CVS Group is 9.9% above its 50D MA; Medicover, Spire Healthcare and Diagnostyka sit within 1.2% of theirs, and Terveystalo and Aevis Victoria are below.
  • Newsflow and company guidance: Terveystalo's guidance cut (20-Oct) is the only dated cohort development in the sources read; Spire Healthcare's takeover is expected to complete in late 2026 or early 2027 (Sector Digest, 22-Oct).

What the peers imply: for CVS Group's direction, watch Pets at Home and Vimian rather than the cohort. For the Sell Criteria, the cohort's slide is the risk to Sector RS, which fell 7 points this week and reads against CVS Group's Stock RS at the 30-Nov month-end (item 11 (the stock against each Sell Criterion)).
