  • Consumer staples' Industry RS: 79 at 13-Nov, from 73 (6-Nov), 59 (16-Oct), 33 (2-Oct) and 20 (10-Oct-2025); 49 points above the line of 30
  • Food production's Sector RS: 51 at 13-Nov, from 57 (6-Nov), 44 (10-Nov), 56 (30-Oct) and 62 (16-Oct); it has fallen 11 points in four weeks while the industry rose 20

The order of the turn: Hilton Food first and alone (July to September), then the industry (October), with the sector lagging. The industry's October and November strength has gone to Food production's large defensive members and the laggards, not to Hilton Food: over the month Danone rose 14.0% (Stock RS 13 to 41), Orkla 10.7% (17 to 33) and Nestle 8.2% (45 to 62), against Hilton Food's 3.3%, and in the week Danone rose 4.0%, Associated British Foods 3.2% and Nestle 2.2% while Hilton Food fell 0.2% (Cranswick and Bell Food Group, also leaders, rose 2.7% and 2.3%). The Industry Digest's de-grossing hypothesis of 30-Oct ("staples falling on days Industrials rise supports de-grossing") has not been borne out: Industry RS has since risen from 66 to 79.

Hypothesis, the likeliest reading of Hilton Food's lag this week: rotation within staples to the laggards and large defensives, not news. Hilton Food's Stock RS rests on its three to twelve-month gain, while over one month it is lagging its industry; if the defensive rotation continues without it, its Stock RS fades slowly, from a long way above its lines. Precedent: Food production's Sector RS has crossed 50 repeatedly this year; the current spell above it is five Fridays (16-Oct to 13-Nov), and the 29-Oct (48) and 10-Nov (44) dips both recovered within days.

4. Consensus by named FY

Analysts raised Hilton Food's EBITDA for all three years on 4-Nov and trimmed two of the three years on 12-Nov; over three months the nearer year has risen fastest, so they are lifting the base, not the trajectory. Hilton Food's financial year ends in late December.

  • EPS: FY2026 52.0p, up 1.1% in a month, 6.5% in three and 9.3% in six; FY2027 56.7p, up 0.6%, 5.6% and 7.7%; FY2028 62.7p, up 0.3%, 3.9% and 4.9%. Each is still about 17% to 18% below its level of a year ago, before the 11-Nov-2025 profit warning
  • Revenue: FY2026 £4,609m, FY2027 £4,801m, FY2028 £5,000m, each up 1.4% to 1.6% in a month, 5% to 6% in three and 9% to 10% in six, as raw-material prices pass through at cost
  • EBITDA: FY2026 £147.5m, FY2027 £158.7m, FY2028 £168.8m, up 2.7%, 2.2% and 2.9% in a month. The 4-Nov step (+2.9%, +2.7% and +2.6%) was partly reversed on 12-Nov ((1.5%), (1.1%) and +0.4%), when FY2026 and FY2027 EPS also fell 0.4% while FY2028 EPS rose 1.0% and FY2028 revenue 0.7%

The implied EBITDA margin is now rising, after falling through the summer. FY2027 EBITDA against revenue is 3.31%, from 3.28% at the September month-end and 3.26% on 8-Oct; FY2026 is 3.20% and FY2028 3.38%. The implied growth is EPS +9.0% into FY2027 and +10.6% into FY2028, on revenue growth of about 4.2% a year, so the case rests on margin and finance costs, not volume alone.

The brokers' range is wide: FY2027 EPS 59.8p at Deutsche Bank and 52.1p at Shore Capital, against consensus of 56.7p (AlphaSense research, filed 13-Nov). The SP has led the estimates: since the 31-Jul month-end it has risen 22.5%, FY2027 EPS 5.6%. Test: the January 2027 trading update and the Walmart Canada launch, the next points where estimates can catch up with the SP.

5 and 6. Analyst ratings, PT, and Net Upgrade Breadth

Hilton Food's ratings have followed its SP rather than led it. Four or five Buys, with at most one other rating, through most of the 2025 decline from 905p (20-May-2025), except the August 2025 month-end (2 Buys, no other rating); Buys fell to 3 at the November 2025 month-end, after the profit warning, and to 2 at the July and August 2026 month-ends while the SP rose 16%; they recovered to 4 at the September month-end, after the 3-Sep results. Coverage fell from 6 or 7 analysts in 2024 and 2025 to 4 in July 2026; the latest counts (13-Nov) are 4 Buys, 1 other, no Sells.

Hilton Food's average PT is 791p, 7.8% above the 733.5p close; the gap has stayed narrow since September. The gap was 29.4% at the June month-end, 3.9% at the September month-end (the narrowest since at least August 2024) and 9.6% on 27-Oct. Named PTs: Deutsche Bank 850p from 815p, Buy (3-Sep, earnings review); Berenberg 810p from 790p (Hargreaves Lansdown, 7-Sep).

The 12-Nov changes look like an analyst returning to the count, not a cut. On 12-Nov the average PT fell from 795p to 791p while PT Net Upgrade Breadth rose from +50% to +60%, its count of analysts from 4 to 5 and its raises from 2 to 3; FY2027 EPS breadth moved from 3 raising and 1 cutting of 5 to 4 and 1 of 6, and the rating counts gained one "other". Hypothesis: a broker with a Hold rating re-entered the data with FY2026 and FY2027 estimates below consensus but FY2028 above it (FY2028 EPS rose 1.0% and FY2028 EBITDA 0.4% that day), lowering the near-year averages; if the average PT moved only on that entry, its PT is about 775p. The higher FY2028 figure fits a broker expecting a later, steeper ramp-up, not a cut to the case. The broker is not named in the sources read. Test: the month-end counts at 30-Nov.

Net Upgrade Breadth is positive on every measure: FY2027 EPS +50% (4 raising, 1 cutting, of 6), EBITDA +50% (+83% at the September month-end, so fading), revenue +50%, PT +60% (3 of 5), Buys 4 of 5.

Where analysts are cutting: one analyst has cut FY2027 EPS since the September month-end; the sources read do not name it. The most cautious broker on file is Shore Capital, which, in AlphaSense's summary, sees Foppen as "a persistent drag" and says the main growth projects "will not contribute before the financial year 2027F" (AlphaSense research, filed 13-Nov). Deutsche Bank (5-Nov) adds that without United States Food and Drug Administration approval Foppen would fetch only £10m to £20m.

9. Peers

Hilton Food led its cohort into November; this month the cohort has caught up while Hilton Food paused. The peer table on the PMS Per-stock Dashboard shows every cohort peer above its 50D MA and every Stock RS higher than a month ago.

  • SP technicals: over the month to 13-Nov Greencore rose 10.1%, Bell Food Group 8.0%, Cranswick 7.9% and LDC 7.4%, against Hilton Food's 3.3%; Hilton Food's close is 3.2% above its 50D MA against Greencore's 11.9% and Bell Food Group's 8.5%. In the week Cranswick rose 2.7% and Bell Food Group 2.3%, Hilton Food (0.2%)
  • Who turned first: Hilton Food (above its absolute 200D MA from 15-Jul), then Bell Food Group (13-Aug), Greencore (October) and, last, Cranswick and LDC, whose Stock RS rose from 34 and 38 to 53 and 51 in a month
  • Sell-side estimates: Hilton Food's three-month EPS revision (+6%) and revenue Net Upgrade Breadth (+50%) are the cohort's best; Greencore +3% and +11%, Cranswick +1% and 0%, Bell Food Group 0% and 0%, LDC (1%) and (25%)
  • Newsflow: Deutsche Bank's Cranswick first-half preview (10-Nov) expects revenue +4.7% to £1,537m and adjusted operating profit +9.5% to £123.7m despite a 14% fall in pig prices; for LDC and Bell Food Group the sources read hold no disclosures since 1-Oct
  • Customers and company guidance: Tesco's take-home grocery sales rose 2.2% year on year in October from 1.0% in September (Deutsche Bank, 10-Nov); against that, Australian cattle prices ended October at AUD 5.40/kg, up 13% in a month (Rabobank, 4-Nov), a cost Hilton Food passes through but with a lag. Hilton Food's own guidance (£66.0m to £71.0m FY2026 adjusted profit before tax, raised 3-Sep) is unchanged

Hilton Food now trades apart from the cohort's daily moves. On 11-Nov Hilton Food fell with its cohort (Cranswick (1.6%), Greencore (1.3%), LDC (0.9%)); on 12 and 13-Nov it fell 0.9% and 0.1% while Cranswick rose 0.5% and 1.6% and Consumer staples 0.5% and 0.8%. Associated British Foods, the stock outside the cohort whose daily moves track Hilton Food's most closely (correlation 0.34, moderate), rose 3.2% in the week. What the peers imply: the cohort's estimates and newsflow are mostly positive (LDC's are not) and its charts are improving, which supports the cohort rather than threatens Hilton Food. Test: whether Hilton Food holds above its rising 20D MA through Cranswick's first-half results and Greencore's full-year results (1-Dec); a fall in Hilton Food on days the cohort rises would suggest holders are selling Hilton Food to buy the laggards.

The data faults

  • AlphaSense weekly report not read (sources/alphasense-weekly.md): the browser pane showed the AlphaSense sign-in form and no Chrome browser was connected. Effect: no brokers' newsflow this week; several sections say the cause of a move or the name of a broker cutting is not in the sources read (Volex PT cuts, Avanza's +4.4% on 13-Nov, Bally's Intralot's 12-Nov fall, Sdiptech's FY2027 EPS cutter).
  • Clock mismatch: the device clock read Sunday 15-Nov during the run, while the scheduler dated it Monday 16-Nov and book.json says it was generated 2026-11-16 06:13. Effect: none on the readings (all of the 13-Nov close); the note's title uses the device date.
  • Volume on 13-Nov differs between the data file and the chart file for Volex (1,800,625 against 1,925,626), Arteche (12,517 against 12,942) and Fevertree (341,078 against 344,078). Effect: small; the sections use the chart file.
  • Distances measured from the close, not from the line, in the 200-day line rule block and the daily classification file (Theon: 16.95% against 14.5% on the line's basis; Volex (6.98%) against (6.5%); Carlsberg and Helios Towers similarly). Effect: the sections state their basis.
  • Xvivo's 20D stop-loss line took effect on 11-Nov with the SP 1.9% below it, against the rule that a moving average may be a stop-loss line only when the price is above it on the day of investment; counted from 23-Oct, the exit would have been confirmed that same day (the 23-Oct close of SEK 206.0 was below the stop-loss of SEK 207.0, the 20D MA of SEK 220.20 less 6%). Xvivo's order_state also reads "PROBING BET" for a Speculative Bet.
  • Arteche's consensus, ratings and Net Upgrade Breadth changed on 11 and 13-Nov because coverage fell from 3 analysts to 1, not because of revisions.
  • Sdiptech: the data file's events show a 30-Oct tranche bought and sold the same day, then "promoted" on 4-Nov; the FY2026 EPS cut is dated 13-Nov in the key days and 5-Nov in the daily series; the Position Management System's per-tranche lines (150D and 200D) are not applied by the data file. Avanza and IMCD show the same mismatch between sources/class-check.md and the data file's lines.
  • Avanza's input section 10 (add stage) used the 16-Nov bar, a day after the readings date.
  • Helios Towers: the 30%-fall line reads "close is 30.0% above it" where the level is 30% below the close; tranche 1 is dated 22-Jun in the data file against your ruling of 15-May; Cellnex's 3-month EPS revision reads (143%).
  • Bally's Intralot: the chart file rounds to EUR 0.01, which distorts the 5-session up/down volume ratio (0.69 against 2.19 unrounded); the data file has filled holiday rows that enter its MAs.
  • Briefs disagree on item 12: WRITER.md asks for a broadening item that SHARED.md section 5 no longer has; the writers followed SHARED.md.
  • book.json carries 6 warnings: the monthly sell-side EPS, EBITDA and sales series for Helios Towers and Volex are not joined (no monthly value within 40 days before the first daily reading). Effect: those two holdings have no monthly sell-side series in book.json; the sections read their consensus from the daily series.
  • Stray scratch files in the run folder (a notes copy and scratch-THEON-NL/joined-draft.md.tmp) could not be deleted (deletes are not allowed in the connected folder); harmless.
